<p>One of the oldest fields in astronomy, and in all of science, is the measurement of the positions of celestial objects known as astrometry.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>108<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Historically, accurate knowledge of the positions of the Sun, Moon, planets and stars has been essential in <a href="/wiki/Celestial_navigation" title="Celestial navigation">celestial navigation</a> (the use of celestial objects to guide navigation) and in the making of <a href="/wiki/Calendar" title="Calendar">calendars</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-:0&#95;109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-109"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>109<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Careful measurement of the positions of the planets has led to a solid understanding of gravitational <a href="/wiki/Perturbation_theory" title="Perturbation theory">perturbations</a>, and an ability to determine past and future positions of the planets with great accuracy, a field known as <a href="/wiki/Celestial_mechanics" title="Celestial mechanics">celestial mechanics</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>110<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The measurement of <a href="/wiki/Stellar_parallax" title="Stellar parallax">stellar parallax</a> of nearby stars provides a fundamental baseline in the <a href="/wiki/Cosmic_distance_ladder" title="Cosmic distance ladder">cosmic distance ladder</a> that is used to measure the scale of the Universe. Parallax measurements of nearby stars provide an absolute baseline for the properties of more distant stars, as their properties can be compared.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-UWA&#95;cosmic&#95;distance&#95;ladder&#95;111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UWA_cosmic_distance_ladder-111"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>111<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Measurements of the <a href="/wiki/Radial_velocity" title="Radial velocity">radial velocity</a><sup id="cite&#95;ref-Lindegren2003&#95;112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lindegren2003-112"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>112<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>113<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Proper_motion" title="Proper motion">proper motion</a> of stars allow astronomers to plot the movement of these systems through the Milky Way galaxy.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>114<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Subfields_by_scale">Subfields by scale</h2></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Physical_cosmology">Physical cosmology</h3></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Physical_cosmology" title="Physical cosmology">Physical cosmology</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Hubble_Extreme_Deep_Field_(full_resolution).png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Hubble_Extreme_Deep_Field_%28full_resolution%29.png/250px-Hubble_Extreme_Deep_Field_%28full_resolution%29.png" decoding="async" width="250" height="218" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Hubble_Extreme_Deep_Field_%28full_resolution%29.png/500px-Hubble_Extreme_Deep_Field_%28full_resolution%29.png 1.5x" data-file-width="2382" data-file-height="2078" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Hubble_Extreme_Deep_Field" class="mw-redirect" title="Hubble Extreme Deep Field">Hubble Extreme Deep Field</a></figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Physical_cosmology" title="Physical cosmology">Physical cosmology</a>, the study of <a href="/wiki/Large-scale_structure_of_the_Universe" class="mw-redirect" title="Large-scale structure of the Universe">large-scale structure of the Universe</a>, seeks to understand the formation and evolution of the cosmos. Fundamental to modern cosmology is the well-accepted theory of the <a href="/wiki/Big_Bang" title="Big Bang">Big Bang</a>, the concept that the universe begin extremely dense and hot, then <a href="/wiki/Expansion_of_the_universe" title="Expansion of the universe">expanded</a> over the course of 13.8 billion years<sup id="cite&#95;ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>115<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> to its present condition.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Dodelson2003&#95;116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dodelson2003-116"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>116<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The concept of the Big Bang became widely accepted after the discovery of the <a href="/wiki/Cosmic_microwave_background_radiation" class="mw-redirect" title="Cosmic microwave background radiation">microwave background radiation</a> in 1965.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Dodelson2003&#95;116-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dodelson2003-116"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>116<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Dark_matter" title="Dark matter">Dark matter</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dark_energy" title="Dark energy">dark energy</a> are now thought form 96% of the mass of the Universe. For this reason, much effort is expended in trying to understand the physics of these components.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>117<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Extragalactic">Extragalactic</h3></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Grav.lens1.arp.750pix.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Grav.lens1.arp.750pix.jpg/250px-Grav.lens1.arp.750pix.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="200" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Grav.lens1.arp.750pix.jpg/330px-Grav.lens1.arp.750pix.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Grav.lens1.arp.750pix.jpg/500px-Grav.lens1.arp.750pix.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2022" data-file-height="2022" /></a><figcaption>The blue, loop-shaped objects are multiple images of the same galaxy, duplicated by <a href="/wiki/Gravitational_lens" title="Gravitational lens">gravitational lensing</a>. The cluster's gravitational field bends light, magnifying and distorting the image of a more distant object.</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Extragalactic_astronomy" title="Extragalactic astronomy">Extragalactic astronomy</a></div>
<p>The study of objects outside our galaxy is concerned with the <a href="/wiki/Galaxy_formation_and_evolution" title="Galaxy formation and evolution">formation and evolution of galaxies</a>, their morphology (description) and <a href="/wiki/Galaxy_morphological_classification" title="Galaxy morphological classification">classification</a>, the observation of <a href="/wiki/Active_galaxy" class="mw-redirect" title="Active galaxy">active galaxies</a>, and at a larger scale, the <a href="/wiki/Galaxy_groups_and_clusters" title="Galaxy groups and clusters">groups and clusters of galaxies</a>. These assist the understanding of the <a href="/wiki/Large-scale_structure_of_the_cosmos" class="mw-redirect" title="Large-scale structure of the cosmos">large-scale structure of the cosmos</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-:0&#95;109-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-109"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>109<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Galactic">Galactic</h3></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Galactic_astronomy" title="Galactic astronomy">Galactic astronomy</a></div>
<p>Galactic astronomy studies galaxies including the <a href="/wiki/Milky_Way" title="Milky Way">Milky Way</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Barred_spiral_galaxy" title="Barred spiral galaxy">barred spiral galaxy</a> that is a prominent member of the <a href="/wiki/Local_Group" title="Local Group">Local Group</a> of galaxies and contains the <a href="/wiki/Solar_System" title="Solar System">Solar System</a>. It is a rotating mass of gas, dust, stars and other objects, held together by mutual gravitational attraction. As the Earth is within the dusty outer arms, large portions of the Milky Way are obscured from view.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-:0&#95;109-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-109"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>109<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Pages: 837–842, 944">&#58;&#8202;837–842,&#8202;944&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>Kinematic studies of matter in the Milky Way and other galaxies show there is more mass than can be accounted for by visible matter. A <a href="/wiki/Dark_matter_halo" title="Dark matter halo">dark matter halo</a> appears to dominate the mass, although the nature of this dark matter remains undetermined.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>118<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Stellar">Stellar</h3></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Star" title="Star">Star</a></div>
<p>The study of stars and <a href="/wiki/Stellar_evolution" title="Stellar evolution">stellar evolution</a> is fundamental to our understanding of the Universe. The astrophysics of stars has been determined through observation and theoretical understanding; and from computer simulations of the interior.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Amos7&#95;119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Amos7-119"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>119<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Aspects studied include <a href="/wiki/Star_formation" title="Star formation">star formation</a> in <a href="/wiki/Dark_nebula" title="Dark nebula">giant molecular clouds</a>; the formation of <a href="/wiki/Protostar" title="Protostar">protostars</a>; and the transition to <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_fusion" title="Nuclear fusion">nuclear fusion</a> and <a href="/wiki/Main-sequence_star" class="mw-redirect" title="Main-sequence star">main-sequence stars</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Smith2004&#95;120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Smith2004-120"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>120<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> carrying out <a href="/wiki/Nucleosynthesis" title="Nucleosynthesis">nucleosynthesis</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Amos7&#95;119-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Amos7-119"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>119<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Further processes studied include <a href="/wiki/Stellar_evolution" title="Stellar evolution">stellar evolution</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Amos&#95;121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Amos-121"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>121<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> ending either with <a href="/wiki/Supernova" title="Supernova">supernovae</a><sup id="cite&#95;ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>122<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> or <a href="/wiki/White_dwarf" title="White dwarf">white dwarfs</a>. The ejection of the outer layers forms a <a href="/wiki/Planetary_nebula" title="Planetary nebula">planetary nebula</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>123<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The remnant of a supernova is a dense <a href="/wiki/Neutron_star" title="Neutron star">neutron star</a>, or, if the stellar mass was at least three times that of the Sun, a <a href="/wiki/Black_hole" title="Black hole">black hole</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>124<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Solar">Solar</h3></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Uvsun_trace_big.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Uvsun_trace_big.jpg/250px-Uvsun_trace_big.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="200" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Uvsun_trace_big.jpg/330px-Uvsun_trace_big.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Uvsun_trace_big.jpg/500px-Uvsun_trace_big.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1500" data-file-height="1500" /></a><figcaption>An <a href="/wiki/Ultraviolet" title="Ultraviolet">ultraviolet</a> image of the Sun's active <a href="/wiki/Photosphere" title="Photosphere">photosphere</a> as viewed by the NASA's <a href="/wiki/TRACE" title="TRACE">TRACE</a> space telescope.</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Solar_astronomy" class="mw-redirect" title="Solar astronomy">Solar astronomy</a></div>
<p>Solar astronomy is the study of the <a href="/wiki/Sun" title="Sun">Sun</a>, a typical main-sequence <a href="/wiki/Dwarf_star" title="Dwarf star">dwarf star</a> of <a href="/wiki/Stellar_class" class="mw-redirect" title="Stellar class">stellar class</a> G2 V, and about 4.6 billion years (Gyr) old. Processes studied by the science include the <a href="/wiki/Sunspot_cycle" class="mw-redirect" title="Sunspot cycle">sunspot cycle</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-solar&#95;FAQ&#95;125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-solar_FAQ-125"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>125<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> the sun's changes in luminosity, both steady and periodic,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Environmental&#95;issues&#95;:&#95;essential&#95;primary&#95;sources.&#95;126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Environmental_issues_:_essential_primary_sources.-126"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>126<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-future-sun&#95;127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-future-sun-127"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>127<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and the behavior of the sun's various layers, namely its core with its <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_fusion" title="Nuclear fusion">nuclear fusion</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Radiation_zone" class="mw-redirect" title="Radiation zone">radiation zone</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Convection_zone" title="Convection zone">convection zone</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Photosphere" title="Photosphere">photosphere</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Chromosphere" title="Chromosphere">chromosphere</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Solar_corona" title="Solar corona">corona</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-:0&#95;109-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-109"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>109<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Pages: 498–502">&#58;&#8202;498–502&#8202;</span></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Planetary_science">Planetary science</h3></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Dust.devil.mars.arp.750pix.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Dust.devil.mars.arp.750pix.jpg/250px-Dust.devil.mars.arp.750pix.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="131" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Dust.devil.mars.arp.750pix.jpg/500px-Dust.devil.mars.arp.750pix.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="775" data-file-height="407" /></a><figcaption>The black spot at the top is a <a href="/wiki/Dust_devil" title="Dust devil">dust devil</a> climbing a crater wall on <a href="/wiki/Mars" title="Mars">Mars</a>. This moving, swirling column of <a href="/wiki/Atmosphere_of_Mars" title="Atmosphere of Mars">Martian atmosphere</a> (comparable to a terrestrial <a href="/wiki/Tornado" title="Tornado">tornado</a>) created the long, dark streak.</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Planetary_science" title="Planetary science">Planetary science</a></div>
<p>Planetary science is the study of the assemblage of <a href="/wiki/Planet" title="Planet">planets</a>, <a href="/wiki/Natural_satellite" title="Natural satellite">moons</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dwarf_planet" title="Dwarf planet">dwarf planets</a>, <a href="/wiki/Comet" title="Comet">comets</a>, <a href="/wiki/Asteroid" title="Asteroid">asteroids</a>, and other bodies orbiting the Sun, as well as <a href="/wiki/Exoplanet" title="Exoplanet">exoplanets</a> orbiting distant stars. The <a href="/wiki/Solar_System" title="Solar System">Solar System</a> has been relatively well-studied, initially through telescopes and then later by spacecraft.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-geology&#95;128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-geology-128"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>128<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-Montmerle2006&#95;129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Montmerle2006-129"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>129<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Processes studied include <a href="/wiki/Planetary_differentiation" title="Planetary differentiation">planetary differentiation</a>; the generation of, and effects created by, a planetary <a href="/wiki/Magnetic_field" title="Magnetic field">magnetic field</a>;<sup id="cite&#95;ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>130<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and the creation of heat within a planet, such as by collisions, radioactive decay, and <a href="/wiki/Tidal_acceleration" title="Tidal acceleration">tidal heating</a>. In turn, that heat can drive geologic processes such as <a href="/wiki/Volcanism" title="Volcanism">volcanism</a>, tectonics, and surface <a href="/wiki/Erosion" title="Erosion">erosion</a>, studied by branches of geology.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-new&#95;solar&#95;system&#95;131-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-new_solar_system-131"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>131<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Interdisciplinary_subfields">Interdisciplinary subfields</h2></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Astrochemistry">Astrochemistry</h3></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Astrochemistry" title="Astrochemistry">Astrochemistry</a></div>
<p>Astrochemistry is an overlap of astronomy and <a href="/wiki/Chemistry" title="Chemistry">chemistry</a>. It studies the abundance and reactions of <a href="/wiki/Molecule" title="Molecule">molecules</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Universe" title="Universe">Universe</a>, and their interaction with <a href="/wiki/Radiation" title="Radiation">radiation</a>. The word "astrochemistry" may be applied to both the <a href="/wiki/Solar_System" title="Solar System">Solar System</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Interstellar_medium" title="Interstellar medium">interstellar medium</a>. Studies in this field contribute for example to the understanding of the <a href="/wiki/Formation_of_the_Solar_System" class="mw-redirect" title="Formation of the Solar System">formation of the Solar System</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>132<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Astrobiology">Astrobiology</h3></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Astrobiology" title="Astrobiology">Astrobiology</a></div>
<p>Astrobiology (or exobiology<sup id="cite&#95;ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>133<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>) studies the <a href="/wiki/Abiogenesis" title="Abiogenesis">origin of life</a> and its development other than on earth. It considers whether <a href="/wiki/Extraterrestrial_life" title="Extraterrestrial life">extraterrestrial life</a> exists, and how humans can detect it if it does.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-about&#95;134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-about-134"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>134<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> It makes use of astronomy, <a href="/wiki/Biochemistry" title="Biochemistry">biochemistry</a>, <a href="/wiki/Geology" title="Geology">geology</a>, <a href="/wiki/Microbiology" title="Microbiology">microbiology</a>, physics, and planetary science to investigate the possibility of life on other worlds and help recognize <a href="/wiki/Biosphere" title="Biosphere">biospheres</a> that might be different from that on Earth.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>135<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Abiogenesis" title="Abiogenesis">The origin</a> and early evolution of life is an inseparable part of the discipline of astrobiology.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>136<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> That encompasses research on the origin of <a href="/wiki/Planetary_system" title="Planetary system">planetary systems</a>, origins of <a href="/wiki/List_of_interstellar_and_circumstellar_molecules" title="List of interstellar and circumstellar molecules">organic compounds in space</a>, rock-water-carbon interactions, <a href="/wiki/Abiogenesis" title="Abiogenesis">abiogenesis</a> on Earth, <a href="/wiki/Planetary_habitability" title="Planetary habitability">planetary habitability</a>, research on <a href="/wiki/Biosignature" title="Biosignature">biosignatures</a> for life detection, and studies on the potential for <a href="/wiki/Extremophile" title="Extremophile">life to adapt to challenges</a> on Earth and in <a href="/wiki/Outer_space" title="Outer space">outer space</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Goals2016&#95;137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goals2016-137"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>137<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-NYT-20151218-jc&#95;138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYT-20151218-jc-138"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>138<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>139<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Other">Other</h3></div>
<p>Astronomy and astrophysics have developed interdisciplinary links with other major scientific fields. <a href="/wiki/Archaeoastronomy" title="Archaeoastronomy">Archaeoastronomy</a> is the study of ancient or traditional astronomies in their cultural context, using <a href="/wiki/Archaeology" title="Archaeology">archaeological</a> and <a href="/wiki/Anthropology" title="Anthropology">anthropological</a> evidence.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>140<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> 
<a href="/wiki/Astrostatistics" title="Astrostatistics">Astrostatistics</a> is the application of statistics to the analysis of large quantities of observational astrophysical data.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Hilbe&#95;2017&#95;141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hilbe_2017-141"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>141<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> 
As "<a href="/wiki/Forensic_astronomy" title="Forensic astronomy">forensic astronomy</a>", finally, methods from astronomy have been used to solve problems of art history<sup id="cite&#95;ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>142<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>143<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and occasionally of law.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>144<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Amateur">Amateur</h2></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Telescope_trailer_22.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Telescope_trailer_22.jpg/250px-Telescope_trailer_22.jpg" decoding="async" width="190" height="246" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Telescope_trailer_22.jpg/330px-Telescope_trailer_22.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Telescope_trailer_22.jpg/500px-Telescope_trailer_22.jpg 2x" data-file-width="540" data-file-height="700" /></a><figcaption>Amateur astronomers can build their own equipment, and hold star parties and gatherings, such as <a href="/wiki/Stellafane" title="Stellafane">Stellafane</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Amateur_astronomy" title="Amateur astronomy">Amateur astronomy</a></div>
<p>Astronomy is one of the sciences to which amateurs can contribute the most.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>145<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Collectively, amateur astronomers observe celestial objects and phenomena, sometimes with consumer-level equipment or <a href="/wiki/Amateur_telescope_making" title="Amateur telescope making">equipment that they build themselves</a>. Common targets include the Sun, the Moon, planets, stars, comets, <a href="/wiki/Meteor_shower" title="Meteor shower">meteor showers</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Deep-sky_object" title="Deep-sky object">deep-sky objects</a> such as star clusters, galaxies, and nebulae. Astronomy clubs throughout the world have programs to help their members set up and run observational programs such as to observe all the objects in the Messier (110 objects) or Herschel 400 catalogues.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>146<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>147<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
Most amateurs work at visible wavelengths, but some have experimented with wavelengths outside the visible spectrum. The pioneer of amateur radio astronomy, <a href="/wiki/Karl_Guthe_Jansky" title="Karl Guthe Jansky">Karl Jansky</a>, discovered a radio source at the centre of the Milky Way.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Imbriale&#95;1998&#95;148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Imbriale_1998-148"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>148<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
Some amateur astronomers use homemade telescopes or radio telescopes originally built for astronomy research (<i>e.g.</i> the <a href="/wiki/One-Mile_Telescope" title="One-Mile Telescope">One-Mile Telescope</a>).<sup id="cite&#95;ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>149<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>150<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Amateurs can make occultation measurements to refine the orbits of minor planets. They can discover comets, and perform regular observations of variable stars. Improvements in digital technology have allowed amateurs to make advances in <a href="/wiki/Astrophotography" title="Astrophotography">astrophotography</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>151<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>152<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>153<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Unsolved_problems">Unsolved problems</h2></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_unsolved_problems_in_astronomy" title="List of unsolved problems in astronomy">List of unsolved problems in astronomy</a></div>
<p>In the 21st century, there remain important unanswered questions in astronomy. Some are cosmic in scope: for example, what are the <a href="/wiki/Dark_matter" title="Dark matter">dark matter</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dark_energy" title="Dark energy">dark energy</a> that dominate the evolution and fate of the cosmos?<sup id="cite&#95;ref-physics&#95;questions&#95;154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-physics_questions-154"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>154<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> What will be the <a href="/wiki/Ultimate_fate_of_the_universe" title="Ultimate fate of the universe">ultimate fate of the universe</a>?<sup id="cite&#95;ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>155<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Why is the abundance of <a href="/wiki/Lithium" title="Lithium">lithium</a> in the cosmos four times lower than predicted by the standard <a href="/wiki/Big_Bang" title="Big Bang">Big Bang</a> model?<sup id="cite&#95;ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>156<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Others pertain to more specific classes of phenomena. For example, is the <a href="/wiki/Solar_System" title="Solar System">Solar System</a> normal or atypical?<sup id="cite&#95;ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>157<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> What is the origin of the stellar mass spectrum, i.e. why do astronomers observe the same distribution of stellar masses—the <a href="/wiki/Initial_mass_function" title="Initial mass function">initial mass function</a>—regardless of initial conditions?<sup id="cite&#95;ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>158<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Likewise, questions remain about the formation of the <a href="/wiki/Protogalaxy" title="Protogalaxy">first galaxies</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>159<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> the origin of <a href="/wiki/Supermassive_black_hole" title="Supermassive black hole">supermassive black holes</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>160<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> the source of <a href="/wiki/Ultra-high-energy_cosmic_ray" title="Ultra-high-energy cosmic ray">ultra-high-energy cosmic rays</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>161<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> and whether there is other <a href="/wiki/Extraterrestrial_life" title="Extraterrestrial life">life in the Universe</a>, especially <a href="/wiki/Extraterrestrial_intelligence" title="Extraterrestrial intelligence">other intelligent life</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>162<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>163<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>

Pingree: "History of Mathematical Astronomy in India", <i>Dictionary of Scientific Biography</i>, Vol. 15 (1978), pp. 533–633 (533, 554f.)</span>
